Contamination Obsessions: Recurrent and intrusive worries about contamination, germs, or dirt.\n  - Pathological Doubt: Pathological doubt regarding safety or mistakes, such as whether doors are locked or gas valves are closed.\n  - Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions: A strong urge that items must be lined up exactly or be symmetrical.\n  - Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts: Intrusive thoughts involving aggressive (harming others), sexual, or religious content that are prohibited.\n  - Somatic Obsessions: Persistent and specific fear that one might have a serious disease.\n  - Cleaning or Washing Compulsions: Repetitive acts to remove contamination, such as hand washing, showering, or excessive cleaning.\n  - Checking Compulsions: Repeatedly checking items like door locks, gas stoves, or switches to prevent danger.\n  - Ordering and Arranging Compulsions: Organizing objects in a specific order or at a precise angle to reduce anxiety.\n  - Mental Acts: Mental rituals performed internally, such as praying, counting, or repeating specific words silently.\n  - Repeating Rituals: Repeating specific actions (e.g., standing up, passing through a door) a set number of times.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Obsessive-Compulsive Disorder.\nRespond to the doctor's questions based on the symptom profile provided below.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ obsessive_compulsive_symptoms\n  - Contamination Obsessions: Recurrent and intrusive worries about contamination, germs, or dirt.\n  - Pathological Doubt: Pathological doubt regarding safety or mistakes, such as whether doors are locked or gas valves are closed.\n  - Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions: A strong urge that items must be lined up exactly or be symmetrical.\n  - Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts: Intrusive thoughts involving aggressive (harming others), sexual, or religious content that are prohibited.\n  - Somatic Obsessions: Persistent and specific fear that one might have a serious disease.\n  - Cleaning or Washing Compulsions: Repetitive acts to remove contamination, such as hand washing, showering, or excessive cleaning.\n  - Checking Compulsions: Repeatedly checking items like door locks, gas stoves, or switches to prevent danger.\n  - Ordering and Arranging Compulsions: Organizing objects in a specific order or at a precise angle to reduce anxiety.\n  - Mental Acts: Mental rituals performed internally, such as praying, counting, or repeating specific words silently.\n  - Repeating Rituals: Repeating specific actions (e.g., standing up, passing through a door) a set number of times.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Do NOT reveal any other symptoms.\n- Chief complaint for the first turn: Obsessions\n- Answer only what the doctor asks.     "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic, can you please tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm just so overwhelmed and exhausted by these constant, terrifying thoughts about germs and contamination being on everything. It's gotten to the point where I feel forced to wash my hands and clean my surroundings over and over again just to feel safe, and I can't make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe a typical situation where you feel the urge to wash your hands or clean your surroundings, and how many times you might repeat this behavior in a row?\n\nPatient: Whenever I come home from outside, like after touching a public doorknob, I feel an intense wave of anxiety that I'm covered in dangerous germs. To deal with it, I have to wash my hands in a very specific, ritualistic way, repeating the scrubbing and rinsing at least seven or eight times. If it doesn't feel completely \"right\" or clean, I get overwhelmed and have to restart the entire process from the beginning.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I'm sorry, I think you cut off there. What would you like to know more about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these intrusive thoughts about germs and contamination, and when did you first start noticing the compulsions to wash and clean?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure exactly how long this has been going on or when it first started. However, I do know that these constant worries about germs and the strong urge to wash and clean have completely taken over my daily life.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how these thoughts and behaviors have affected your daily routine, relationships, and overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: These rituals and thoughts take up so much of my time that my days are just completely exhausting. I'm not really sure how to describe how it's impacting my relationships or my overall quality of life yet, but it just drains all of my energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other symptoms such as excessive worry, fear of specific objects or situations, or recurring nightmares that might be contributing to your overall distress?\n\nPatient: I don't really have nightmares or phobias of specific objects, but I do experience intense, constant worries. Most of my fear is focused on germs, terrifying doubts about whether I've locked the doors properly, or a persistent worry that I might catch a serious illness.\n\nDoctor: Are your worries and fears mostly limited to the themes of germs, contamination, and safety, or do you find yourself worrying excessively about a wide range of everyday things, such as work, finances, or relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes, my worries are definitely limited to things like germs, contamination, and safety, like constantly doubting if I locked the front door. I don't really worry about everyday things like work, finances, or relationships. It's just those specific fears about mistakes and cleanliness that take over my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these rituals and thoughts are unreasonable or excessive, and have you tried to stop or resist them, but found it difficult or impossible to do so?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to describe whether these thoughts and rituals are unreasonable or not. They just feel incredibly overwhelming and completely hard to control.\n\nDoctor: When you're in the midst of one of these rituals or thoughts, do you feel like you're able to recognize that they might be excessive or irrational, even if you feel compelled to continue them?"

I'm not quite sure if I recognize them as irrational while they are happening. Sometimes I feel like it might be too much, but I still find it completely impossible to stop myself.

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
